Even when I don't have anything to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like a big drop in how much you eat or a noticeable loss of weight without trying?\n\nPatient: No... my appetite is fine. I haven't lost any weight. Everything's just... normal with that.\n\nDoctor: When you feel this restlessness, do you find yourself making repetitive movements, like tapping your feet or fidgeting, or do you just get up and move around?\n\nPatient: I guess... I try to stop, but... I don't always realize I'm doing it until the ladies sitting next to me start looking over.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic, needed much less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I sleep fine. My thoughts are just... normal, I guess. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel a lack of energy or feel fatigued, even when you haven’t done much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I feel tired sometimes, like anyone else. But I wouldn't say I'm always fatigued or lacking energy for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness happen in all situations, like at home, in meetings, or when you're out with friends, or is it only in certain places or times?\n\nPatient: I suppose... it's mostly everywhere. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.0, F90.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. Can you tell me what’s been going on that brought you in today?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I'm here because I just can't seem to settle down. It's like I can't be comfortable in my own skin anymore. Even when I'm completely worn out and know I should just sit still, I feel this... urge to jump up and do the next thing. It's just constant.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what you mean by not being comfortable in your own skin? Does it feel like you can't relax or rest, even when you want to?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... like I just said. I just can't relax. Even if I'm dead tired and I know I need to just sit in my chair, I can't stay put. I just feel like I have to get up again. I don't really know how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how long this feeling of needing to get up and move around has been going on? Has it been happening for most days, or is it something that comes and goes?\n\nPatient: It happens most days. I guess... well, it's been causing real trouble since my husband died. That was about a year and ten months ago.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband passed away, have you been feeling down or depressed most days, or has it mostly just been this restlessness that you described?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I wouldn't say I'm depressed. Just... restless, like I said. I just can't seem to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t bring you the same pleasure anymore?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I still like seeing my friends and doing things. It's just... well, I just have a hard time sitting still when I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have thoughts that keep coming back, that you can't seem to get rid of, even if you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I don't really get stuck on things like that. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge, like something bad might happen, even when there’s no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't feel anxious. Just... always needing to be doing something, I guess. But not scared of anything bad happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually excited or high, like you had more energy than usual or needed less sleep?\n\nPatient: No... I don't get like that. I don't feel high or anything. I just... I sleep fine, mostly. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what that restlessness feels like inside? Is it more of a physical feeling, like you just can’t sit still, or more of a mental feeling, like your thoughts are racing or you can’t settle your mind?\n\nPatient: It's... I don't know. I guess I just feel like I can't settle inside my own skin anymore. \n\nEven if I'm completely worn out... and I know I really should just be sitting still... I get this feeling like I have to jump up and do the next thing. So... mostly physical, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: When you feel this need to get up and move, is it usually connected to something specific you need to do, or does it feel more like a general inability to stay still even when there’s no task or purpose?\n\nPatient: I don't know... it's just a feeling. Not really connected to anything. I just... can't stay still. Even when I don't have anything to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like a big drop in how much you eat or a noticeable loss of weight without trying?\n\nPatient: No... my appetite is fine. I haven't lost any weight. Everything's just... normal with that.\n\nDoctor: When you feel this restlessness, do you find yourself making repetitive movements, like tapping your feet or fidgeting, or do you just get up and move around?\n\nPatient: I guess... I try to stop, but... I don't always realize I'm doing it until the ladies sitting next to me start looking over.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic, needed much less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I sleep fine. My thoughts are just... normal, I guess. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel a lack of energy or feel fatigued, even when you haven’t done much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I feel tired sometimes, like anyone else. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if this restlessness has been a long-standing issue for you, even going back to when you were younger, or is it something that really started later in life?\n\nPatient: I suppose it's... been around a long time.
